  2. The server runs the verifier and stores the verdict itself; the save request only names that verdict and answers each flag. Pro: the client cannot invent a verdict; matches the brief and D-80. Con: new tables, endpoints, tests, and a verifier hook.
  3. Only a database trigger. Pro: cannot be bypassed from the API. Con: cannot express "edited and re-verified" easily.
- What we chose: Option 2, plus the database trigger from option 3 as a backstop (both).
- Why: The save gate is the safety heart of the project, so it must not trust the client. The trigger means even someone writing SQL directly cannot mark a record saved without a stored verdict and a confirmation for every flag.
- What we actually did: New tables `clinic.verdicts` and `clinic.record_resolutions` (forced RLS, append-only triggers, no UPDATE/DELETE grants; resolutions have no free-text column). `POST /records/{id}/verify` runs the verifier on the stored record and stores the verdict, with a record hash computed by the database. `POST /records/{id}/save` takes `{verdict_id, resolutions:[{check_id, field_path, action}]}` (extra fields refused, so no "by"). Rules: physician only; resolver = token user; every flag in the stored verdict has exactly one resolution (missing 409, unknown or duplicate 422); stale verdict 409; "edited" needs a changed record, a new stored verdict, and no flag the physician did not confirm; resolutions, status, approver, audit rows in one transaction. Trigger `records_saved_gate` (sql/050_gate.sql). Files: m5_backend/app/main.py, models.py, verifier.py, sql/010, 020, 050, tests/test_save_gate.py (SG-1 replay refused), 13 new matrix cases. Run `uv run pytest m5_backend/tests/test_save_gate.py`.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: m5 cannot import the m4 verifier, so the real verifier is wired in at integration through `create_app(verifier=...)`. Until then the built-in default flags the whole record and the physician must confirm it: that is a click, not a safety check. A physician holding raw database credentials could insert a forged verdict (RLS lets her write verdicts for her facility); the API never does. Reviewers may still click "confirm" without looking (automation bias). The optional extra step for critical flags (audio playback, second confirmation) is NOT built. Audit condition 6 (a "reason" text) is not built, so there is no new free text. The old red/green order was followed when writing tests, but the red run was only observed for the pool fix (F5); the others were written first and passed after the code.
- What else could be done: Require audio playback for critical checks (C3, C4); add a short reason code list (not free text); let the verifier run as its own database role so even physician SQL cannot insert verdicts.
- How to change this decision later: app/main.py (save_record, verify_record), sql/050_gate.sql, tests/test_save_gate.py; m6_ui must change its save call (see CONTRACT.md).
- Status: done (m6_ui adaptation and real verifier wiring pending at integration)

### D-95: The audit log takes the role from the database session, not from a setting   (Phase B / m5_backend / backend-security-engineer / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: F2: the audit function read `app.role` and `app.user_id`, which any session running SQL can change, so a researcher could write a row saying "physician".
- Options we considered: (1) leave it as documented residual risk. Pro: no work. Con: weakens the audit trail as evidence. (2) Read the role from the session role (`current_setting('role')`) and check that the user id exists with that role. Pro: cheap forgery stops. Con: a session with raw `api_login` credentials can still `SET ROLE` to another app role. (3) One database login per app role. Pro: strongest. Con: contradicts the brief's single api_login design and needs five pools.
- What we chose: Option 2.
- Why: It closes the audit attempt AL-forge with a small change and keeps the brief's design.
- What we actually did: `app.audit_write` in sql/040_audit.sql derives the role from the session role and refuses a user id that does not exist with that role; the `app.role` setting is no longer set or read. Test: tests/test_hardening.py::test_f2_researcher_cannot_write_an_audit_row_as_physician.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Raw `api_login` credentials still allow choosing any app role (residual limit, same as the GUC limit). The maint role (operator) may write rows with any existing user id.
- What else could be done: Per-role database logins; sign audit rows.
- How to change this decision later: sql/040_audit.sql, tests/test_hardening.py.
- Status: done

### D-96: Research export drops groups smaller than 5 patients   (Phase B / m5_backend / backend-security-engineer / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: F3: with age band, sex, month, visit type and a pseudonym, almost every patient in the export was unique.
- Options we considered: (1) leave it. Pro: simplest. Con: easy to single people out. (2) Drop every group (age band x sex x month x visit type) with fewer than k = 5 distinct patients. Pro: simple, explainable. Con: loses rare groups, not full anonymity. (3) Merge small groups into wider ones. Pro: keeps more rows. Con: more logic to get wrong.
- What we chose: Option 2 with k = 5, and a `k_min` argument for tests.
- Why: Simple and checkable. We accept data loss over a false sense of privacy.
- What we actually did: `suppress_small_cells` in app/export.py. The seed gets a cohort of 5 consenting patients (C1..C5) in one cell; the other three consenting patients are alone in their cells and are NOT exported. So the synthetic export holds 5 patients (the cohort), nothing else. Tests: tests/test_hardening.py (pure and database), updated tests/test_research_export_db.py; the matrix points research cases at cohort patients.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Symptoms and medicines are not part of the cell, and the pseudonym links one patient's visits, so this is NOT proof of anonymity. k = 5 is a convention, not a calculation.
- What else could be done: Merge small groups; add l-diversity or a formal risk estimate; ask a privacy expert.
- How to change this decision later: K_MIN in app/export.py; seed cohort in seed/seed.py.
- Status: done

### D-97: Withdrawing consent removes research rows at once   (Phase B / m5_backend / backend-security-engineer / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: F4: a withdrawal only took effect when someone re-ran the export; nothing guaranteed that would happen.
- Options we considered: (1) schedule the export. Pro: no schema change. Con: still a delay. (2) A database trigger on `clinic.consents` that deletes the patient's research rows in the same transaction. Pro: immediate. Con: needs a way to find the pseudonym without the secret key in the database. (3) A synchronous job called by an API. Pro: no trigger. Con: direct SQL updates would bypass it.
- What we chose: Option 2 with a private map table `research.pseudonym_map` (patient id to pseudonym), filled by the export, readable only by the operator role.
- Why: It cannot be skipped, and the HMAC key stays out of the database.
- What we actually did: sql/050_gate.sql triggers `consents_withdrawn` and `consents_deleted`; app/export.py fills the map. Test: tests/test_hardening.py::test_f4_withdrawal_removes_research_rows_immediately (no re-export in between).
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: The map is a patient-to-pseudonym link inside the database; whoever holds owner credentials and uses `maint_role` can read it. Re-consent needs the next export to bring the patient back.
- What else could be done: Store a keyed hash of the patient id instead of the id.
- How to change this decision later: sql/050_gate.sql, app/export.py.
- Status: done

### D-98: The connection pool wipes session state on every return   (Phase B / m5_backend / backend-security-engineer / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: F5: a session-level `SET ROLE` or `SET app.*` (only possible through an injection) stayed on the pooled connection for the next user (audit attempt G-2).
- Options we considered: (1) check `current_user = session_user` before reuse. Pro: cheap. Con: misses other settings. (2) `DISCARD ALL` when returning a connection. Pro: resets everything; fail closed if it errors. Con: tiny cost per request; also deletes prepared statements.
- What we chose: Option 2, and automatic statement preparation is switched off.
- Why: Everything session-level is forgotten, whatever was set.
- What we actually did: app/db.py `_put` runs `DISCARD ALL`; `prepare_threshold = None`. Problem hit: with auto-prepare on, the matrix failed with "prepared statement does not exist", because the client kept names the server had discarded. Test: tests/test_hardening.py::test_f5_injected_session_level_set_role_does_not_reach_next_request (seen failing when the reset is removed).
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Not preparing statements costs a little speed.
- What else could be done: Use a real pool library with a reset hook.
- How to change this decision later: app/db.py.
- Status: done

### D-99: The API refuses to start with a dangerous database login   (Phase B / m5_backend / backend-security-engineer / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: F6: nothing stopped the API from running as a superuser, a BYPASSRLS role or the table owner if `API_DB_USER` was set wrongly.
- Options we considered: (1) document it. Con: one wrong line in .env removes all protection. (2) Check at startup and exit. Pro: fails closed. Con: one extra query.
- What we chose: Option 2.
- Why: A silent misconfiguration is the worst failure here.
- What we actually did: `Database.verify_login()` (app/db.py) is called in `create_app`; it refuses superuser, BYPASSRLS, CREATEROLE, CREATEDB, REPLICATION, owning tables or functions in clinic/research/app, or a session role different from the login. Test: tests/test_hardening.py::test_f6_api_refuses_to_start_with_unsafe_login (bootstrap, owner, a BYPASSRLS login refused; api_login accepted).
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: The check runs once at startup; a role changed later is not noticed until restart.
- What else could be done: Re-check periodically.
- How to change this decision later: app/db.py, `check_login` argument of `create_app`.
- Status: done

### D-100: Separate password for owner_role; Docker bootstrap user is mm_bootstrap   (Phase B / m5_backend / backend-security-engineer / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: F7: `.env.example` told Docker users to use `POSTGRES_USER=owner_role`, which would make the image's superuser and owner_role share a name and password.
- Options we considered: (1) only fix the comment. Con: the code still reused one password. (2) New `OWNER_DB_PASSWORD`, `POSTGRES_USER=mm_bootstrap`. Pro: clean separation. Con: existing local .env files keep working only through a legacy rule.
- What we chose: Option 2, with a legacy rule: if `POSTGRES_USER` is still `owner_role`, `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` is used for owner_role (so the existing local setup works).
- Why: One credential per purpose.
- What we actually did: .env.example, docker-compose.yml comments, app/config.py; tests in tests/test_hardening.py (F7). 
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: The Docker path is UNVERIFIED (no Docker here).
- What else could be done: Run the Docker path once; drop the legacy rule.
- How to change this decision later: app/config.py, .env.example.
- Status: partly done (Docker run UNVERIFIED)

### D-101: Pharmacist patient reference is random; the catalog row-count leak is accepted   (Phase B / m5_backend / backend-security-engineer / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: F8: `patient_ref` was the first 8 characters of the patient UUID; and any role can read table statistics and see "Rows Removed by Filter" in EXPLAIN.
- Options we considered: (1) a keyed hash per facility. Con: the database would need a secret. (2) A random per-patient reference stored in `clinic.patients.ref`. Pro: unrelated to the id, no secret. Con: one more column. For the row-count leak: block catalog reads (Con: breaks PostgreSQL tools) or accept.
- What we chose: Option 2 for the reference; ACCEPT the row-count leak as a documented residual limit.
- Why: The leak needs raw SQL, which the API never runs; hiding statistics would break normal PostgreSQL behaviour.
- What we actually did: sql/010 (`ref` column), sql/030 (view joins patients through one narrow policy). Test: tests/test_hardening.py::test_f8_patient_ref_is_not_a_uuid_prefix. README "Limits".
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Anyone with raw SQL can still learn approximate table sizes.
- What else could be done: Per-facility keyed references; revoke `pg_stat` views from app roles.
- How to change this decision later: sql/010, sql/030.
- Status: done (row-count leak accepted)

### D-102: Tests no longer rewrite tracked result files; one foreign-key snag   (Phase B / m5_backend / backend-security-engineer / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: The audit noted that running the tests rewrote `results/m5_backend/access_matrix_summary.json`. Also: revoking UPDATE from owner_role on `verdicts` broke the foreign key from resolutions.
- Options we considered: tests write results (con: noisy diffs) or only the CLI does; for the snag, keep UPDATE for the owner on verdicts (the trigger still refuses) or add a special column grant.
- What we chose: `test_access_matrix` runs with `write=False`; `make m5-matrix` writes the three results files. owner_role keeps UPDATE on `verdicts` only (PostgreSQL's foreign-key check needs it); the append-only trigger still blocks every update.
- Why: Clean diffs; the trigger is the real guard.
- What we actually did: tests/test_access_matrix.py, sql/050_gate.sql. Results regenerated once: 1499 cases, 0 unauthorized successes of 1398 deny-expected, 3/1398 = 0.0021, 0 unexpected denials.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: A superuser or owner can disable triggers (same residual as the audit log). The expected-access YAML gained one hand-written line (`attacks.save_gate: deny`); the same AI wrote it, so a human should re-read it. access_case.v1 has no `save_gate` attack value, so those cases use `mass_assignment` / `id_guess` (contract request in open_questions.md).
- What else could be done: Ask for a `save_gate` attack value in access_case.v1.
- How to change this decision later: tests/test_access_matrix.py, sql/050_gate.sql, matrix/generate.py.
- Status: done

### D-103: Checking the m5 fix pass and what is still open   (Phase B / m5_backend / orchestrator / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: The m5 fix pass (Sonnet, per D-65) reported all eight security findings (D-92) and the confirmed-flag rule (D-80) fixed. Do we accept it, and what is left?
- Options we considered: (1) Trust the report and commit. Pro: fast. Con: the brief says never present unverified work as done. (2) Re-run the tests ourselves with the database up, run the full repository test suite (the builder only ran the m5 target), run lint, then commit; leave a deeper Opus security re-check as an optional next step. Pro: real evidence at low cost. Con: we did not re-attack the new code ourselves.
- What we chose: Option 2.
- Why: Running the tests is cheap and catches broken work; a full new audit costs much more usage.
- What we actually did: Started the sandbox database and ran `uv run pytest m5_backend/tests`: 146 passed; the save-gate subset: 22 passed (including the replay of attack SG-1, now refused). Stopped the database. `mingw32-make test` on the whole repository: 639 passed, 91 skipped, exit 0. `mingw32-make lint` first FAILED on formatting in 11 files (10 from this fix pass, 1 m4 test file); fixed with `ruff format` (formatting only), re-ran the affected tests (61 passed, 87 skipped without the database) and lint (passes). Access matrix after the fix: 0 unauthorized successes of 1398 deny-expected cases (1499 total), rule-of-three upper bound 3/1398 = 0.0021, 0 unexpected denials.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Only the F5 test was seen failing on the old code; the other new tests were written first but never run against the old code, so we do not know they would have caught the old bugs. The real m4 verifier is not yet plugged into m5 (m5 may not import m4); until integration, m5's default verifier flags every record for manual review. m6_ui still sends the old save request and must be adapted in integration. The optional extra confirmation step for CRITICAL flags (audit condition 8) is not built. The research export now keeps only cells with at least 5 patients (k = 5); with our small synthetic seed it holds just 5 patients (a seed cohort added for this), and the other 3 consenting patients are suppressed. access_case.v1 has no "save_gate" attack value, so those cases are labelled mass_assignment / id_guess; a contract version bump is requested (open question). The same AI wrote the rules, tests and matrix.
- What else could be done: A short Opus security re-check of the new save gate (`m5_backend/app/main.py` save_record and `m5_backend/sql/050_gate.sql`); bump access_case to v2 with a save_gate attack type.
- How to change this decision later: m5_backend/; Phase C integration wires m4 into m5 and adapts m6.
- Status: done (open items listed above)

### D-104: Child doses rewritten: syrup in ml or no dose   (Phase B / m1_data / data-scientist / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: Templates a3, e8, h2, h3, i5 showed a child ("ಮಗುವಿಗೆ") getting an adult tablet dose (1000 mg, 2 tablets, 400 mg). How do we fix the text and the gold labels?
- Options we considered:
  1. Give the child turn no dose at all. Pro: never invents a number. Con: the paediatric dose case is no longer tested.
  2. Use a paediatric syrup in ml (the Kannada reviewer's wording, "ಸಿರಪ್ ಐದು ಎಂ.ಎಲ್"). Pro: realistic, tests the ml unit. Con: the ml volumes (2.5, 5, 7.5, 10) are synthetic and NOT checked against an age band.
  3. Delete the child templates. Pro: simple. Con: loses the case where dosing danger is highest.
- What we chose: a mix. a3 (caregiver says what was given) has no dose ("Paracetamol ಸಿರಪ್ ಕೊಟ್ಟೆ"). e8, h2, h3, i5 use paracetamol syrup in ml with verb ಕೊಡಿ. New slot kind `dose: child_syrup` (only ml) separate from `dose: given` (adult, never ml).
- Why: A child must never get an adult dose in our own gold labels. We did not add an age band because we have no fetched age table and must not invent doses.
- What we actually did: data/templates/{a,e,h,i}_*.yaml, m1_data/generate.py, render.py, kn.py (ML_WORDS), lexicon.py/lexicon_source.py (syrup_ml hint), split.py (ml combinations alternate separately so both splits have two). Test: m1_data/tests/test_refreeze1.py. Per changed template (conversations): a3 dev 8, e8 dev 16, i5 dev 8, h2 test 8, h3 test 8, e2 test 16. Zinc 20 mg dispersible tablet in e7 stays (it is the paediatric dose per the WHO page the reviewer fetched).
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: ml volumes are synthetic and not age-checked (UNVERIFIED, not dosing advice). Kannada wording is ai_simulated_review only, not native-validated. m4 has no verified ml range, so it will flag these (intended).
- What else could be done: a fetched paediatric table (IAP or NLEM) so age band and ml can be paired and checked.
- How to change this decision later: data/templates, Drug.syrup_ml hint in m1_data/lexicon_source.py, then `uv run python -m m1_data generate --refreeze` (only before the first test run).
- Status: done (needs human review of the Kannada)

### D-105: Lexicon corrections from the clinical review   (Phase B / m1_data / data-scientist / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: Which lexicon rows to change after the simulated clinical review (D-91).
- Options we considered: (1) change only rows backed by a page the reviewer fetched; (2) change everything the reviewer suggested including unfetched values. We chose (1).
- What we chose: paracetamol 500-1000 mg, max daily 4000 (NHS page); ondansetron 4-24; metformin 500-1000; ibuprofen 200-800 (openFDA OTC + Rx labels); ORS note: unit is sachet or ml, never mg; iron-folic acid note: iron in mg, folic acid in mcg; alias "625" added to amoxicillin-clavulanate; alias "levo" and Kannada ಲೇವೋ removed from levofloxacin; ORS Kannada spelling now ಓ.ಆರ್.ಎಸ್ (long O). Metronidazole, cotrimoxazole and NLEM rows unchanged and still UNVERIFIED.
- Why: no dose range without a fetched source. The CSV has no unit column (other modules read it), so ORS and iron units are recorded in verified_by, not invented as numbers.
- What we actually did: m1_data/lexicon_source.py, lexicon.py; `uv run --extra data python -m m1_data build-lexicon`; data/lexicon/drugs.csv (8 rows changed). Numeric alias "625" is kept out of generated speech.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Ranges come from US/UK pages, not from NLEM (fetch failed). 1000 mg metformin single dose is a derivation. "625" may be read by m3 as a dose number. The reviewer is simulated.
- What else could be done: real pharmacist fills the clinical sheet; add a unit column through a contract change.
- How to change this decision later: m1_data/lexicon_source.py then rebuild lexicon and refreeze only before a test run.
- Status: partly done (human check pending)

### D-106: Shared negation table v2   (Phase B / m1_data / data-scientist / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: Apply the D-93 table changes inside the same refreeze, because the table is checked during generation.
- Options we considered: (1) edit now, inside REFREEZE-1; (2) edit later and refreeze again. Option 2 would change the test lock twice.
- What we chose: Option 1. Version 1 -> 2: soft cue now needs a bare consonant before -ಲ್ಲ (ಬೆಲ್ಲ jaggery no longer matches; also in exclude list and a trap entry NEG-T14); new soft cue NEG-S07 for the negative question -ಲ್ವಾ (marked generate: false, ಅಲ್ವಾ stays a trap); ಆಗ added to the -ದೆ stems (ಆಗದೆ); clause splitter no longer splits at a dot directly followed by a letter (ಎಂ.ಜಿ).
- Why: fixes over-flagging and missed cues; traps must stay at zero false triggers.
- What we actually did: .claude/skills/kannada-nlp/negation_table.yaml; m1_data/coverage.py skips `generate: false` entries; tests in test_refreeze1.py (every trap gives zero triggers). Form "ಕೊಡುವುದು ಬೇಡಿ" removed from templates (ungrammatical); the whole-token cue NEG-W05 is kept alive by the spaced artefact "ಕೊಡ ಬೇಡಿ".
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: not native-validated. m3 and m4 may need updates (m3 reads the table, new type use is soft_suffix). The full-stop rule fails for a full stop directly followed by a letter.
- What else could be done: native reviewer rules on negative questions and their answers.
- How to change this decision later: edit the table, bump version, add a decision.
- Status: done

### D-107: Three new CRITICAL severity classes   (Phase B / m1_data / data-scientist / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: Add the clinical reviewer's missing critical classes.
- Options we considered: add now (before any test run) or leave out. Adding after a test run would be goalpost moving.
- What we chose: add CR-ROUTE (wrong route), CR-PAEDIATRIC (child or weight-based dose treated as adult), CR-LIQUID-STRENGTH (mg per 5 ml or ml errors).
- Why: these are real dosing dangers not covered by CR-DOSE.
- What we actually did: docs/severity.md, marked "added before any test run, REFREEZE-1 (D-91)". Test in test_refreeze1.py.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: from a simulated clinician; m7/m4 must implement the classes.
- What else could be done: real clinician review.
- How to change this decision later: docs/severity.md plus a new decision.
- Status: done

### D-108: Annotation guide no longer prints test-only words   (Phase B / m1_data / data-scientist / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: The guide printed 8 words found only in test templates (integrity finding M1-6). Builders read the guide, so it leaked test wording.
- Options we considered: mark them as test words (still leaks) or rewrite with dev-family examples only.
- What we chose: rewrite; plus a rule and a test.
- Why: a leak channel should be closed by construction.
- What we actually did: docs/annotation_guide.md rewritten examples (also ಓ.ಆರ್.ಎಸ್, ml rule, spoken variants); tests test_guide_has_no_test_only_vocabulary (computed from the current split plan) and test_guide_does_not_print_the_eight_leaked_words.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: the test covers template vocabulary only, not wording of other docs. Optional ಸಲ for "times" was NOT generated (changing it would reshuffle all data); recorded as open.
- What else could be done: apply the same check to other docs builders read.
- How to change this decision later: docs/annotation_guide.md and m1_data/tests/test_refreeze1.py.
- Status: done

### D-109: REFREEZE-1 carried out   (Phase B / m1_data / data-scientist / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: Regenerate and relock the frozen test split once (D-91).
- Options we considered: see D-91.
- What we chose: one `--refreeze` after all fixes. Confirmed first that results/test_access_log.md does not exist (no test run ever).
- Why: fixes unsafe child doses before any evaluation.
- What we actually did: generator version m1-data-1.0.0 -> m1-data-1.1.0; `uv run python -m m1_data generate --refreeze`. Old test.lock sha256 05abfb0e15abade0ab184e90b4f023802271cee1aa198c82d36f806e67ac8403; new ef214fecd70e748f2dc7725d4e1259ec8c4396fad85ad19c15d74476fa7e9e59 (532 lines). Counts unchanged: 592 conversations, dev 328, test 264, test medication entries 352, twins dev 224 / test 193. Second plain run identical. No family or dosed combination overlap (tests pass). Added drug-conflict rule (quinolone/doxycycline with zinc/iron, two NSAIDs or steroids not co-dosed) and e2 patient line "ಎರಡೂ ಔಷಧಿ ಒಟ್ಟಿಗೆ ಕೊಡಬಹುದಾ?"; positive twins of h5, e7, h2 changed to sensible sentences.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: still looks like moving the goalposts; the old lock is in git history. Test paracetamol ml volumes are only 5 and 7.5.
- What else could be done: none before first eval-test.
- How to change this decision later: never after the first make eval-test.
- Status: done (orchestrator commits)

### D-110: REFREEZE-1 checked by the orchestrator; what is still open   (Phase B / m1_data / orchestrator / 2026-11-10)
- What we needed to decide: The m1 fix pass (Sonnet, per D-65) changed the data and relocked the test split. Do we accept it and commit it as the single REFREEZE-1 commit?
- Options we considered: (1) Accept and commit now. Pro: the test data changes exactly once, before any test run. Con: a few small items stay open. (2) Send it back for another round first. Pro: fewer open items. Con: costs another agent run, and the open items do not need test data to change again.
- What we chose: Option 1.
- Why: Every check passed when the orchestrator re-ran it independently, and no test result exists (results/test_access_log.md is absent).
- What we actually did: Re-ran the checks myself. A second plain `generate` left the working tree byte-identical; `split-lock: OK`; 592/592 gold valid (gold.v1), 592/592 transcripts valid, 1186/1186 manifest lines valid, 79 fixtures OK; ruff clean; full pytest 669 passed, 91 skipped, 1 failed. Dev/test membership (dev.txt, test.txt) is unchanged; only file contents changed. A scan of all transcripts found 0 child (ಮಗುವಿಗೆ) conversations with a tablet or adult dose. The 160 changed gold files come from three expected causes: ORS respelled ಓ.ಆರ್.ಎಸ್, "ಕೊಡುವುದು ಬೇಡಿ" replaced, and paracetamol doses re-drawn from the corrected 500-1000 mg range (for example 1000 -> 650). The one failing test (m3_extract/tests/test_lexicon.py, ORS short-vowel spelling) is now marked as an expected failure (strict), so it turns red again the moment m3 fixes it, and is assigned to the m3 fix pass, which must still accept the short-vowel form that speech recognition may produce.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: The builder wrote "ಕೊಡ ಬೇಡಿ" (with a space) instead of the reviewer's "ಕೊಡಬೇಡಿ", only to keep one negation cue frequent enough; this is a real but less common spelling, and it means the joined form is not in the data (sent to the human Kannada sheet). Child syrup volumes (2.5-10 ml) are synthetic and not checked against an age table. The negative-question cue (NEG-S07) is in the table but not generated, because nobody has decided how its answer should be labelled. The builder ran `git stash` once by mistake and restored it at once; the lock hash was unchanged afterwards.
- What else could be done: Add a unit column to the drug list (a contract change) instead of keeping units in a notes column.
- How to change this decision later: Only before the first `make eval-test`, with a new decision entry and one commit (as for REFREEZE-1).
- Status: done

### D-111: The verifier only reads dev data, checked by a list of allowed ids, not by folder names   (Phase B / m4_verify / safety-engineer / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: How m4 makes sure its planted-mistake experiment (E4) and its confidence-threshold tuning (tune-c7) never read the frozen TEST split. The old guard (M4-1, CRITICAL in the integrity review) refused a path only if its name contained the word "test". But the test gold files sit in the same folder as the dev files (data/gold/), so `e4 --gold-dir data/gold` would have read all 264 test files.
- Options we considered:
  1. Keep the name check and tell people not to point it at data/gold. Pro: no work. Con: one wrong command leaks the test split; a copied folder with a harmless name passes.
  2. A deny-list: refuse ids in data/splits/test.txt. Pro: simple. Con: a file whose id was changed slips through; under data/ we would still open the test files to read their ids.
  3. An allow-list plus several deny checks: under data/ open a file ONLY if its id is in data/splits/dev.txt; everywhere refuse a document whose id is in test.txt, whose template family is a test family (data/splits/split_plan.json), or whose bytes match a hash in data/splits/test.lock. Pro: test files under data/ are never even opened; copies and renamed files are caught. Con: a test document that was both re-labelled AND edited would still pass (no check can catch everything).
- What we chose: Option 3.
- Why: The test split is the only honest final exam we have. A guard that fails open on one wrong command is not a guard. Checking the id, the family and the file hash costs almost nothing.
- What we actually did: New file m4_verify/split_guard.py (load_gold, read_guarded_json, guard_doc, guard_bytes). planted.load_gold_dir and tune_c7.main now go through it; the old name check (refuse_test_split) is deleted. The CLI prints "error: REFUSED: ..." and exits with code 2. The only way past is ALLOW_TEST=1 together with a non-empty PURPOSE=...; every such read is appended to results/m4_verify/test_access_log.md. Nobody set ALLOW_TEST in this work. Tests: m4_verify/tests/test_split_guard.py (15 tests: data/gold gives exactly the 328 dev ids and no test file is opened; a copied folder, a renamed file, a test family under a new id, a JSONL dump and a hash-identical copy are all refused; missing split lists fail closed; tune-c7 refuses a test transcript by audio id and by data path without opening it; the CLI refuses). These tests never read test content: the "test documents" are fixture files with a test id written into them. Run: `uv run pytest m4_verify/tests/test_split_guard.py -q`.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: A test conversation that is copied, given a new id, moved to a dev family name AND edited by one character would pass. The audit log is m4's own (results/m4_verify/test_access_log.md), not the project log results/test_access_log.md that `make eval-test` writes (m4 may not write outside its folders); see open_questions.md. The `verify` command (one record at a time, used by the app) is not guarded, on purpose: it is not a tuning or evaluation reader.
- What else could be done: Move the guard into a shared helper that every module uses (needs an orchestrator decision, since modules may not import each other); add a CI test that runs every m4 command on data/ and checks the access log stays empty.
- How to change this decision later: m4_verify/split_guard.py and m4_verify/tests/test_split_guard.py.
- Status: done

### D-112: The dose check (C4) never passes a child's dose, and says "cannot verify" apart from "wrong"   (Phase B / m4_verify / safety-engineer / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: The simulated clinical review (reviews/m4_verify/2026-11-10-clinical.md, ai_simulated_review, accepted in D-91) found that C4 compared every dose with the ADULT range, so "paracetamol 500 mg for a child" passed; that "no range in our drug list" looked the same as "dose is wrong"; that every unit (ml, sachet, tablet) was allowed for every drug ("ceftriaxone 1 tablet" or "ORS 500 mg" passed the unit test); and that the maximum daily dose was used only where the drug list has it.
- Options we considered:
  1. Add a children's dose table. Pro: could really check child doses. Con: we have no fetched paediatric source (IAP / NLEM not fetched); inventing doses is forbidden.
  2. Flag every dose in a conversation that mentions a child as "cannot verify" (fail-closed), and split C4 reasons into codes. Pro: safe, honest, cheap. Con: more flags for humans to review; it cannot tell a correct child dose from a wrong one.
  3. Keep one flag type and only fix the child case. Pro: less work. Con: scores would keep mixing gaps in our drug list with real dose errors (alarm fatigue).
- What we chose: Option 2, plus allowed units per drug and the existing max-daily check with its own code.
- Why: A verifier must never certify what it cannot check. Separating "cannot verify" from "wrong" keeps the measurement honest: the scores now show how much review burden comes from our own lexicon gaps.
- What we actually did: m4_verify/checks.py C4: every flag reason now starts with a code and a severity class from docs/severity.md, e.g. `cannot_verify:paediatric [CR-PAEDIATRIC]: ...`. Codes: real violations `span_mismatch`, `unit_not_allowed` (CR-ROUTE for an injection-only drug given as tablet/ml, else CR-DOSE), `out_of_range`, `exceeds_max_daily`; cannot-verify `cannot_verify:paediatric` (a child word such as ಮಗು/ಮಗುವಿಗೆ/ಮಕ್ಕಳ/ಶಿಶು or "child" anywhere in the conversation), `cannot_verify:no_range` (blank range), `cannot_verify:not_in_lexicon`, `cannot_verify:liquid_strength` (dose in ml: no mg-per-5 ml strength known, CR-LIQUID-STRENGTH), `cannot_verify:count_unit` (tablets/capsules: strength not stated). Allowed units: m4 config table m4_verify/config/default.yaml (paracetamol adds ml for syrup; iron-folic acid tablet/mg/mcg; ORS sachet/ml) -> else the dosage form named in the lexicon's verified_by note (tablet, capsule, syrup, sachet, powder for injection: m4_verify/lexicon.py units_from_note) -> else solid oral forms only (mg, g, mcg, tablet, capsule). Max daily uses only data/lexicon/drugs.csv values (paracetamol 4000, ibuprofen 3200, metformin 2550 ...). Tests: m4_verify/tests/test_c4_fixpass.py (written first; before the fix the child 500 mg dose PASSED C4). Helpers `reason_code()` and `severity_class()` let reports count the codes.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: The child word list is AI-written and not native-validated; ಮಗ/ಮಗಳು (son/daughter) are NOT cues because adults are often called that, so a child named only that way is missed. Any child word anywhere flags ALL doses in the conversation (for example an adult's own dose in a visit about a child). Units derived from free-text notes can be wrong if a note mentions a form in passing (amoxicillin-clavulanate gets ml because its note mentions a suspension label). The paracetamol syrup row is UNVERIFIED (no syrup strength fetched). Review burden on error-free dev records goes up; that is shown in the results, not hidden.
- What else could be done: Add age/weight and route fields to record.v2 (contract change), a fetched paediatric dose table, and a unit column in drugs.csv.
- How to change this decision later: m4_verify/checks.py (c4_dose_range), m4_verify/lexicon.py (CHILD_PREFIXES_KN, units_from_note), m4_verify/config/default.yaml (allowed_units, allowed_units_by_drug), and the tests in m4_verify/tests/test_c4_fixpass.py.
- Status: done (clinical values need human check)

### D-113: Planted drug swaps use only look-alike drugs from our drug list   (Phase B / m4_verify / safety-engineer / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: How the planted-mistake generator (E4, type f "drug swap") picks the wrong drug. Before, it could pick a look-alike that is not in our drug list (prednisone, fomepizole, sertraline...), which C2/C4 then caught trivially as "unknown drug", or fall back to a RANDOM drug when no look-alike was listed (clinical review M4-M4).
- Options we considered:
  1. Keep the random fallback. Pro: more swaps. Con: "paracetamol -> metformin" is not a realistic confusion and is easy to catch; it inflates the catch rate.
  2. Only use look-alike pairs from the confusable_with column of data/lexicon/drugs.csv, read in both directions, and only drugs that are in the list. Pro: realistic and honest. Con: drugs with no listed look-alike (paracetamol, ibuprofen, zinc ...) are never swapped.
  3. Add AI-proposed pairs (dexamethasone/prednisolone, diclofenac/ibuprofen). Pro: more coverage. Con: not from a fetched source.
- What we chose: Option 2.
- Why: A planted mistake should look like a real one. Swapping to a drug the verifier does not know measures the drug list, not the verifier.
- What we actually did: m4_verify/planted.py: new `confusables(drug, lex)` (both directions, lexicon drugs only); `_swap_drug` has no fallback; medications whose drug has no confusable are not candidates. The E4 summary reports how many medication items can never be swapped. Test: m4_verify/tests/test_planted.py::test_drug_swaps_pick_only_lexicon_confusables (red before the fix, for both lexicons).
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: The confusable pairs in drugs.csv are mostly AI-proposed name-similarity pairs (UNVERIFIED; the ISMP list could not be read). Paracetamol, the most common drug in our data, is never swapped.
- What else could be done: Fetch a real confusable-names list (ISMP or an Indian source) and add the pairs to drugs.csv through m1.
- How to change this decision later: m4_verify/planted.py (confusables, _swap_drug) and data/lexicon/drugs.csv (confusable_with, via m1).
- Status: done

### D-114: Negation rules: spoken "don't", polite "no", negative questions, clauses without commas, abbreviations   (Phase B / m4_verify / safety-engineer / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: Which of the simulated Kannada reviewer's findings (reviews/m4_verify/2026-11-10-kannada.md, ai_simulated_review, accepted in D-93) to build into m4's OWN negation rules. m4 must not read the shared table .claude/skills/kannada-nlp/negation_table.yaml (independence).
- Options we considered:
  1. Copy the shared table's new version. Pro: one set of rules. Con: a mistake in the table would then be invisible to the verifier (both sides wrong in the same way); not allowed by the brief.
  2. Hand-code each accepted finding in m4_verify/negation.py with a positive sentence, a minimal-pair twin and a trap, written as failing tests first. Pro: independent and testable. Con: AI-written rules, not native-validated.
- What we chose: Option 2.
- Why: Missing "ತಗೋಬೇಡಿ" ("don't take") or reading "ಜ್ವರ ಇದೆ ಕೆಮ್ಮು ಇಲ್ಲ" ("fever yes, cough no") as "no fever" are safety bugs whatever their source.
- What we actually did: m4_verify/negation.py: (j) ಬೇಡ/ಬೇಡಿ/ಬೇಡಾ fused after any letter or sign (ತಗೋಬೇಡಿ, ಕೊಡ್ಬೇಡಿ, ತಿನ್ಬೇಡ; spaced ಕೊಡ ಬೇಡಿ already worked); (k) whole words ಬೇಡಾ, ಇಲ್ರೀ, ಇಲ್ರಿ, ಇಲ್ಲರೀ, ಇಲ್ಲಪ್ಪ, ಇಲ್ಲಮ್ಮ, and ಬೇಡವಾ/ಬೇಡವೇ/ಬೇಡ್ವಾ as negative questions; (l) new soft rule S-LVA for C+ಲ್ವಾ ("won't you ...?"): counted as negation but always flagged by C8 as "polarity uncertain" (ಅಲ್ವಾ, the tag "right?", still never triggers); (m) a clause also ends after a finite predicate (ಇದೆ, ಇಲ್ಲ, -ಿದೆ, -ಿಲ್ಲ) when more words follow without punctuation, except before a quotative (ಅಂತ, ಎಂದು), a tag (ಅಲ್ವಾ) or an address word; (n) a cross-turn answer may be one answer word plus address words ("ಇಲ್ಲ ಅಕ್ಕ", "ಹೌದು ಸರ್", "ಹೌದ್ರೀ"); (o) no clause split at the dot of an abbreviation (ಎಂ.ಜಿ., ಓ.ಆರ್.ಎಸ್., ಡಾ.). Tests: m4_verify/tests/test_negation_fixpass.py (61 tests; 28 failed before the fix: j 4, k 4, l 4, m 4, n 10, o 2). All older negation tests and every trap still pass (zero false triggers). The re-run of the real-text audit (m4_verify/negation_audit.md, last section) gives identical cue output on all 52 Wikipedia sentences.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Not native-validated. The new forms are tested only on AI-written sentences; the real-text audit contains none of them. "-ಬೇಡಿ" fused could also match ಕೈಬೇಡಿ (handcuffs), a rare word. Splitting after ಇದೆ could cut a clause that a native speaker reads as one. Not splitting after an abbreviation dot can merge two real sentences (for example "... 500 mg. ಕೆಮ್ಮು ಇಲ್ಲ."), which makes the dose look negated: this errs on the side of a false flag, not a missed negation. The reviewer's other low-confidence items (ಇಲ್ಲವಾದರೆ, ನಿಲ್ಲಿಸದೆ as positive, ಬಿಲ್ಲವ trap, more clause words) are not done.
- What else could be done: A real Kannada speaker fills reviews/human_sheets/negation_rules_review_sheet.csv; test on transcribed real speech.
- How to change this decision later: m4_verify/negation.py together with m4_verify/tests/test_negation_fixpass.py and test_negation.py.
- Status: done (needs human Kannada review)

### D-115: Honest E4 numbers: a catch must land on the planted field, counts use unique mutations with Wilson intervals, review burden sits next to recall, the second-transcript row is an upper bound   (Phase B / m4_verify / safety-engineer / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: How the planted-mistake experiment (E4) counts and reports results. The simulated integrity review (reviews/m4_verify/2026-11-10-integrity.md, ai_simulated_review, accepted in D-90) found four overclaims: (a) "caught" meant ANY flag anywhere in the record (5 of 30 wrong-patient "catches" were an unrelated paracetamol flag); (b) the README quoted repeated injections ("200/200") instead of unique mutations (for example 4/4, CI 0.51-1.0); (c) the review burden quoted used the friendly fixture lexicon (0/18) instead of the project lexicon (1/18); (d) the "second transcript" condition used the perfect gold text without saying so.
- Options we considered:
  1. Keep "any flag" and add footnotes. Pro: no code change. Con: a noisy verifier would look better; footnotes get lost.
  2. Field-level attribution plus unique counts as the headline, with the review burden on the SAME records printed next to every recall number, and the oracle row renamed. Pro: honest and checkable. Con: smaller-looking numbers and more columns.
- What we chose: Option 2.
- Why: A safety number that can be inflated by unrelated alarms or by repeating the same mutation is not a measurement. Recall without the false-alarm rate on clean records says nothing.
- What we actually did: m4_verify/evaluate.py: `caught` = a flag on the injected field, its item (item-level C7/C8 flag), the other half of the dose pair, or a max-daily flag; record-level "/" flags and other items never count. `detected` = the same but the flag is not cannot_verify and was not already present on the error-free record. `sent_to_review` = any flag (kept only as a secondary column). Every proportion is over UNIQUE mutations with a Wilson 95% interval (hand-checked values in m4_verify/tests/test_evaluate.py); repeats are listed as `n_injections`. New tables per_field.csv and a per_check.csv over unique mutations. The second-transcript condition is now called `oracle_second_transcript_upper_bound` everywhere. C7 now also flags the item whose clause holds a difference (before, only "/"), so field-level credit is possible. E4 was run on m1 DEV gold for the first time (data/gold filtered by dev.txt; 328 conversations; seed 20261109; 1800 record-level and 1600 transcript-level injections; 5528 verdicts, 0 schema errors). Dev results (results/m4_verify/dev/e4/summary.md): review burden on the same error-free records: 271/1200 fields flagged [0.203, 0.250], of which only 63/1200 [0.041, 0.067] are not "cannot verify" (the rest are mostly tablet counts, child doses and blank ranges); record level with encounter index, every type caught at the field, e.g. negation drop 193/193 [0.981, 1.0], digit change 200/200 [0.981, 1.0], digit swap 139/139 [0.973, 1.0], drug swap 185/185 [0.980, 1.0], wrong patient 200/200 [0.981, 1.0] (0/200 [0, 0.019] without the index); transcript level text-only: negation drop 14/193 [0.044, 0.118], drug swap 0/183 [0, 0.021], frequency 0/200, duration 0/200 [0, 0.019]; with the ORACLE second transcript (upper bound only) every unique mutation of all eight types is caught at the field (e.g. negation drop 193/193 [0.981, 1.0]). The fixture results (results/m4_verify/e4 and data_lexicon/e4) were regenerated with the same code.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Planted errors on synthetic, template-generated text are much easier than real mistakes; record-level 100% says the rules can see a value that disagrees with its own span, not that real extractor errors will be caught. The "dev" gold was generated by m1 from templates that m4's vocabulary was partly written against. The oracle row is an upper bound only; C7 with a real second ASR output (with its own errors) is not measured. Remaining clean-record flags come from verifier vocabulary gaps (symptom names chills / tiredness / breathing difficulty and the fused spelling ಮೈಕೈನೋವು are not in m4's symptom list) and from soft negation (ಇಲ್ಲದೆ) and unanswered questions; these are deliberately NOT fitted to the dev labels in this pass. One small vocabulary fix was made after the first dev run: the colloquial ದಿವಸ ("day") was missing from the duration words (the Kannada review had assumed it was there); this halved the non-cannot_verify clean flags (124 -> 63 of 1200 fields) and is disclosed as dev-informed.
- What else could be done: Natural-error evaluation on m3 output (kept separate from planted errors); a real second ASR transcript for C7; add the missing symptom names through a documented, dev-only vocabulary decision.
- How to change this decision later: m4_verify/evaluate.py (at_field, unique_outcomes, run_e4, _summary_md) and m4_verify/tests/test_evaluate.py; re-run with `mingw32-make m4-e4-dev`.
- Status: done

### D-116: m4 fix pass checked by the orchestrator; the honest headline is the text-only row   (Phase B / m4_verify / orchestrator / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: The m4 fix pass (Opus, per D-65) fixed the test-split guard (M4-1) and all accepted review findings, and re-ran E4 on dev. Do we accept it, and which E4 numbers may we quote?
- Options we considered: (1) Quote the record-level catch rates (all near 100%). Pro: they are real. Con: there the error is planted in the record while the transcript stays correct, so comparing the two finds it easily; it says little about errors that come from speech recognition. (2) Quote the text-only transcript row as the headline, next to the review burden. Pro: closest to the real risk (the transcript itself is wrong). Con: the numbers are low.
- What we chose: Accept the fix pass. Headline = text-only transcript row plus review burden; record-level and oracle rows are reported only with their labels.
- Why: The brief forbids overclaiming. When the transcript itself carries the error, rules alone caught only 14 of 193 negation drops and 0 drug, frequency or duration swaps on dev.
- What we actually did: Re-ran checks myself: full pytest 778 passed, 91 skipped, 1 xfailed (the known m3 ORS test); ruff clean; `git diff -- data` empty; results/test_access_log.md absent; 0 test ids in results/m4_verify. Guard demo: copies of test file m1-a5-000 in a scratch folder, once under its own name and once renamed x.json, were both REFUSED (the bytes match test.lock) and no output was written. E4 dev (328 conversations, seed 20261109, unique mutations, Wilson 95% CI): review burden 189/328 error-free records flagged [0.52, 0.63], 48/328 [0.11, 0.19] excluding cannot_verify; fields 271/1200 [0.20, 0.25], 63/1200 [0.04, 0.07]. The builder reported 28 of 61 new negation tests and the child-dose C4 test red before the fix.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: More than half of error-free records go to review, mostly "cannot verify" (count units, child doses, missing ranges); that is safe but tiring for reviewers. The new Kannada rules and the child word list are not native-validated (ai_simulated_review). ದಿವಸ was added after seeing a dev run (dev-informed, disclosed). The wrong-patient check catches nothing without the encounter index (0/200).
- What else could be done: Add unit and paediatric tables from fetched sources to turn many cannot_verify flags into real checks; m7 reports seen-frame and unseen-frame slices on test.
- How to change this decision later: m4_verify/checks.py, m4_verify/config/, then re-run `mingw32-make m4-e4-dev`.
- Status: done

### D-117: Remove test-only words from m3 and add a guard test (D-90 finding M3-1)   (Phase B fix pass / m3_extract / nlp-engineer / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: The integrity audit found words in m3 that occur only in test-family templates (the spoken "if you take" ending ಡ್ರೆ in a regex, ಮುಗಿಸದೆ in the LLM prompt, ಆಂಟಿಬಯಾಟಿಕ್ಗಳು and ಆ in unit tests). What to do, and how to stop it coming back?
- Options we considered:
  1. Keep the words and cite the annotation guide as their source. Pro: no work. Con: the ಡ್ರೆ branch can only ever fire on test text, so it can only help the test score.
  2. Remove them and add an automatic test. Pro: clean and repeatable. Con: tiny loss of coverage for that spoken form.
- What we chose: Option 2.
- Why: A rule that only changes test results is not allowed even if its word has a legitimate source. The test makes the rule permanent.
- What we actually did: Removed the ಡ್ರೆ branch (rules.py), the ಮುಗಿಸದೆ example (llm.py), and reworded 3 unit tests. New m3_extract/tests/test_no_test_only_words.py reads TEMPLATE text only (families that data/splits/split_plan.json maps to test; never a test transcript or test gold), takes away every word that also appears in dev templates, _forms.yaml, DEV gold transcripts or fixtures, and fails if any m3 .py/.yaml file contains what is left, or a fragment found only inside such a word. Shown red by re-adding ಡ್ರೆ, then removed. Run: `uv run pytest m3_extract/tests/test_no_test_only_words.py`.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: DISCLOSURE: this removal was informed by the auditor having seen the test templates, so it is itself a decision made with test-template knowledge. The test cannot detect indirect influence of sentence frames (D-89). The negation table and annotation guide are deliberately not treated as legitimate sources, which may later flag words that really are shared. Not native-validated.
- What else could be done: Have m7 report the three test-only families (a_fever_cough__f3, c_stopped_early__f1, i_traps__f2) as separate slices.
- How to change this decision later: edit m3_extract/tests/test_no_test_only_words.py (sources) and the files it names.
- Status: done

### D-118: Kannada fixes from the language review: ಆಗಿದ್ದರೆ, fused symptoms, ಪ್ಯಾಕೆಟ್ (D-93)   (Phase B fix pass / m3_extract / nlp-engineer / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: Which of the Kannada reviewer findings (label ai_simulated_review) to implement in m3.
- Options we considered:
  1. Implement the three high or medium ones with a red test each. Pro: closes real gaps. Con: the new forms are grammar inference, not native-checked.
  2. Wait for a real speaker. Pro: no wrong rule. Con: the gaps stay and the generator already produces these forms.
- What we chose: Option 1.
- Why: The generator positive twin uses ಆಗಿದ್ದರೆ and sachets are spoken ಪ್ಯಾಕೆಟ್; ignoring them is a known miss.
- What we actually did: (a) phrases.yaml FU-FEVER-DOWN now accepts ಆಗಿದ್ದರೆ. (b) rules.py fused_forms builds ಜ್ವರವಿಲ್ಲ, ಕೆಮ್ಮಿಲ್ಲ, ವಾಂತಿಯಿಲ್ಲ, ಜ್ವರವಿದೆ and so on from each symptom (final ು becomes ಿ, glide ವ or ಯ); a fused word that already carries its own ಇಲ್ಲ no longer borrows the next clause predicate. (c) units.py: ಪ್ಯಾಕೆಟ್ = sachet. Red before green: the new tests in test_rules.py failed first (7 failures), then passed.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: The sandhi rule and ಪ್ಯಾಕೆಟ್ = sachet are UNVERIFIED and not native-validated. ಗುಳಿಗೆ, ಟ್ಯಾಬ್ಲೆಟ್ and inflected ಮಾತ್ರೆಯನ್ನು from the same review are NOT done. A positive fused ಕೆಮ್ಮಿದೆ before a comma may still borrow the next clause.
- What else could be done: a native speaker fills reviews/human_sheets; add the remaining forms.
- How to change this decision later: m3_extract/rules.py (fused_forms), data/phrases.yaml, units.py and their tests.
- Status: partly done

### D-119: Accept both ORS spellings, ಓ.ಆರ್.ಎಸ್ (data) and ಒ.ಆರ್.ಎಸ್ (ASR)   (Phase B fix pass / m3_extract / nlp-engineer / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: REFREEZE-1 respelled ORS in the shared lexicon with the long vowel ಓ; ASR may still output the short vowel ಒ. What should m3 accept?
- Options we considered:
  1. Only the lexicon spelling. Pro: simple. Con: misses ASR output.
  2. Add the short form as an m3-side variant. Pro: both work; data/ stays untouched. Con: one more file to keep in sync.
- What we chose: Option 2.
- Why: We may not edit data/, and a missed ORS mention is a missed medication.
- What we actually did: m3_extract/data/asr_variants.yaml (ors: ಒ.ಆರ್.ಎಸ್, ಒ ಆರ್ ಎಸ್) loaded by lexicon.py as match_type "variant". test_multi_token_names_longest_match covers both; the xfail marker is removed (0 xfail left).
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: That ASR writes the short vowel is a guess; no ASR has been run. Not native-validated.
- What else could be done: ask m1 to add the form to the shared lexicon variants.
- How to change this decision later: edit m3_extract/data/asr_variants.yaml.
- Status: done

### D-120: Rescore on REFREEZE-1 data, child syrup in ml, honest labels, LLM comparison marked STALE   (Phase B fix pass / m3_extract / nlp-engineer / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: After the data was regenerated, how to rescore, which version to freeze, what to do with the old rules-vs-LLM table, and how to word results (D-90 M3-2, M3-3).
- Options we considered:
  1. Rerun the LLM comparison. Pro: a current comparison. Con: about 25 minutes for 40 conversations, over the 15-minute limit.
  2. Mark it STALE and keep the old outputs. Pro: honest and cheap. Con: no current LLM number.
- What we chose: Option 2 for the LLM; rescore the rules on all 328 DEV conversations.
- Why: The old LLM outputs were made on data that no longer exists, so scoring them against the new gold would be wrong.
- What we actually did: Rules rerun on DEV. First result: 10 critical errors (the child syrup dose "ಎರಡೂವರೆ ಎಂ.ಎಲ್" = 2.5 ml was unreadable; red test, then ಎರಡೂವರೆ = 2.5 added) plus 9 major (8 template-i5 conversations whose fever condition sits in the drug clause, and 1 follow-up "ವಾಂತಿ ಇದ್ದರೆ ತಕ್ಷಣ ಬನ್ನಿ"); fixed with dev-informed, in-sample additions (an allow_drug_clause flag and one follow-up pattern). Final: dev_tune critical 0/2704, major 0/2704; dev_holdout critical 0/920, major 48/920; all dev critical 0/3624 (pooled, not quoted as accuracy). A new "field" slice gives per-field rows with N and Wilson CIs. Frozen: results/m3_extract/rules_v3_frozen/ (current code and data); rules_v1_frozen stays as the historical unseen-wording estimate (7.6 per 1000, pre-REFREEZE). Old LLM outputs moved to results/m3_extract/llm_STALE_pre_refreeze/. README and failure_analysis.md say: everything is in-sample, 32 of the 40 comparison conversations are in-sample for the rules, no pooled headline. Child turns get ml doses and no adult tablet dose is invented (test_child_turn_without_dose_gets_no_invented_tablet_dose).
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Wilson CIs treat fields as independent; errors cluster by template, so they are too narrow. The holdout is contaminated. Only gold transcripts, no ASR. ಎರಡೂವರೆ is UNVERIFIED (not fetched). ಒಂದೂವರೆ and other half-numbers are not handled.
- What else could be done: rerun `mingw32-make m3-run-llm-dev` (about 25 min) and read holdout ids only; per-template bootstrap CIs.
- How to change this decision later: re-run `uv run python -m m3_extract run-rules --split dev` and `score`; update m3_extract/failure_analysis.md.
- Status: partly done

### D-121: m3 fix pass checked by the orchestrator; dev numbers are in-sample only   (Phase B / m3_extract / orchestrator / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: The m3 fix pass (Sonnet, per D-65) removed test-only words, added the Kannada forms from D-93, accepted both ORS spellings and re-scored dev on the regenerated data. Do we accept it, and what may we say about the numbers?
- Options we considered: (1) Accept and quote the new dev numbers as the result. Pro: simple. Con: the rules were written and re-fixed on dev, so 0 critical on dev says little about new wording. (2) Accept, but quote dev numbers only as in-sample and keep the older unseen-wording figure visible. Pro: honest. Con: less impressive.
- What we chose: Option 2.
- Why: The rules were fixed after looking at dev failures (including 9 new errors after REFREEZE-1), so dev is in-sample; the only real out-of-sample estimate for m3 will come from the single test run in Phase C.
- What we actually did: Re-ran checks myself: full pytest 796 passed, 91 skipped, 0 failed, 0 xfail; ruff clean; `git diff -- data .claude contracts docs` empty; no test id appears in m3_extract or results/m3_extract; results/test_access_log.md absent; the new test that forbids test-only words passes (the builder showed it fails when ಡ್ರೆ is put back); 328/328 dev records valid against record.v1 (the logs folder holds event logs, not records). README labels all dev numbers in-sample and marks the rules-vs-LLM comparison STALE (pre-REFREEZE data, 32 of 40 conversations in-sample); old LLM outputs moved to results/m3_extract/llm_STALE_pre_refreeze/.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Wilson intervals are too narrow because errors cluster by template. dev_holdout is contaminated (rules were seen against it). The new Kannada forms are not native-validated (ai_simulated_review). The LLM comparison is out of date until rerun (about 25 minutes).
- What else could be done: Rerun the LLM comparison with `mingw32-make m3-run-llm-dev` before the final report; use cluster (per-template) bootstrap intervals in m7.
- How to change this decision later: m3_extract/rules.py and phrase files; re-score dev; never tune on test.
- Status: done

### D-122: Clinical review of m3: the record format cannot hold route, liquid strength or "past vs prescribed"   (Phase B / m3_extract / orchestrator / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: The simulated clinical reviewer (reviews/m3_extract/2026-11-11-clinical.md, label ai_simulated_review; Sonnet per D-65) found 3 critical, 8 major and 4 minor problems. Most come from the record format (record.v1), not from m3's rules. Where do we fix them?
- Options we considered: (1) Patch m3 only. Pro: small. Con: m3 cannot output a field the contract does not have (route, strength), so the critical gaps stay. (2) A contract bump to record.v2 by the architect, then m3, m4, m5, m6 and the scorer follow. Pro: fixes the root cause. Con: touches every module; costs one architect run and small follow-ups. (3) Do nothing now and list them as limitations. Pro: free. Con: "0 critical" on dev would hide error kinds the scorer cannot even see.
- What we chose: Option 2 for the critical items and the clearly structural majors; everything else disclosed. Until the bump, every m3 number must say the scorer cannot produce CR-ROUTE, CR-PAEDIATRIC or CR-LIQUID-STRENGTH.
- Why: A record that says "ceftriaxone 1000 mg" without "injection", or "10 ml" without the syrup strength, can mislead a prescriber; a missing field is a safety gap, not a style issue.
- What we actually did: Validated the report (valid review_report.v1) and confirmed the human clinical sheet only gained rows. Accepted for record.v2 (architect, start of Phase C, before m7): route; liquid strength (mg per 5 ml) and patient age band; strength separate from quantity; as-needed and food timing on the medication row; a status field that separates prescribed / already taken / advised against / stopped early (replacing the overloaded negated:true); speaker. Then m3 extracts them and the scorer can emit the three new critical classes. Accepted for docs/severity.md (before any test run): advice and follow-up error codes, with a missed return-precaution considered for CRITICAL. Accepted for m3 later: "once every two days", ಒಂದೂವರೆ, tests for combination products, context for ಚಮಚ and ಪ್ಯಾಕೆಟ್, a symptom duration must not be read as course length, label the stale LLM text. Sent to a human: template e4 says omeprazole "after food" while the fetched openFDA label says before meals (a data change is only allowed before the first test run).
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: A contract bump late in Phase B can break modules that were already reviewed; integration QA must re-check every boundary. The reviewer had no shell and did not run the extractor; "code read" findings are UNVERIFIED.
- What else could be done: Keep record.v1 and add an optional "extensions" object instead of a version bump (less clean, but not a breaking change).
- How to change this decision later: contracts/schemas (record.v1 -> record.v2, architect), then m3_extract, m4_verify, m5_backend, m6_ui and the m7 scorer.
- Status: planned

### D-123: Security audit of m6: role is only a click, and live mode can save a record the reviewer never saw   (Phase B / m6_ui / orchestrator / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: The simulated security auditor (reviews/m6_ui/2026-11-11-security.md, label ai_simulated_review; Opus per D-65) made 31 attempts and found 2 major and 6 minor problems. When do we fix them?
- Options we considered: (1) A separate m6 security fix pass now. Pro: the gap closes sooner. Con: m6 must change again in Phase C anyway for the new /save body (D-94), so two passes would touch the same code. (2) Fold the fixes into the Phase C m6 adaptation, as one m6 pass together with the Kannada fixes. Pro: one pass, one re-test. Con: the gap stays open a little longer.
- What we chose: Option 2, with these findings as acceptance tests for that pass.
- Why: Both major findings are in "live mode", which only matters once m6 talks to the real m5 in Phase C; m5's own database rules (row-level security, the save gate) already stop the worst outcome on the server.
- What we actually did: Validated the report (valid review_report.v1); confirmed nothing is listening on ports 8501, 8599 or 54329 and no repo file changed. Accepted all findings. F1: each browser user logs in as their own m5 user (one mock token per person); the role comes from the token, never from a click; the ASHA view shows only her own patients. F2: live mode loads the record it shows from m5 by the same id it saves. F3: use /save with the gate and drop the separate /approve call, or make /approve run the same check (small m5 change). F4: disable edit and approve after a save. F5: the mock gate cannot be switched off from the page. F6: reject Unicode bidi control characters (U+202A-202E, U+2066-2069) in record text (validator, with record.v2). F7: the mock verifier needs a minimum drug-name check. F8 (low confidence): add anti-framing headers if Streamlit allows it.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Live mode was only read in code, not run against m5; gitleaks was not installed (a regex scan plus exact matching of .env values was used instead). Both must be run in the final audit (C2) and before the tag.
- What else could be done: Put a small reverse proxy in front of Streamlit to add headers; out of scope for a local class project.
- How to change this decision later: m6_ui/app.py and its live client; m5_backend/app/main.py for /approve.
- Status: planned

### D-124: Kannada review of the m6 screen text: 12 wording fixes, and no text yet for the new safety flags   (Phase B / m6_ui / orchestrator / 2026-11-11)
- What we needed to decide: The simulated Kannada reviewer (reviews/m6_ui/2026-11-11-kannada.md, label ai_simulated_review; Sonnet per D-65) read all 157 screen strings and found 12 major and 12 minor problems. Which do we accept?
- Options we considered: (1) Accept everything now. Pro: one round. Con: no web source was fetched; all findings are model knowledge, low confidence. (2) Accept the safety-text majors and the missing keys, and send everything to the native-review sheet. Pro: fixes what could mislead a reviewer. Con: wording may still be imperfect.
- What we chose: Option 2, fixed in the same Phase C m6 pass as the security findings.
- Why: Text such as "ಪರಿಶೀಲಕ: ಸರಿ" reads as if the system certifies the record is correct, which the brief forbids us to imply; a negation warning that does not say the meaning may be reversed under-warns.
- What we actually did: Validated the report (valid review_report.v1); confirmed the Kannada human sheet only gained rows. Accepted for the m6 pass: reason.C3 must say the meaning may be reversed; reason.C4 gets a subject; badge.ok must not sound like a correctness guarantee; one consistent word each for "flagged", "marked" and "speech recognition"; fix approve.ready word order, fail.blind_spot (ಬಿಟ್ಟರೆ; add ಬೇಡ), the mock banner (add "do not use for treatment"), the "open flag" calque; make help.not_clinician and help.clinician clearly different; add keys for child dose, cannot verify, route and max daily (wording checked against the clinical sheet); remove the English word in error.load. Minor wording goes to the native sheet.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Every proposal is model knowledge only; dialects and phone rendering of ZWNJ words are untested.
- What else could be done: A short session where two native-speaking clinic staff read the screens aloud (in HUMAN_TODO).
- How to change this decision later: m6_ui/i18n/kn.json and en.json.
- Status: planned

### D-125: Add record.v2 and gold.v2 as NEW versions next to v1 (route, strength, formulation, age, status...)   (Phase C / contracts / architect / 2026-11-12)
- What we needed to decide: The simulated clinical review of m3 (D-122) showed that our record format (record.v1) has no place for the route ("injection" or "by mouth"), the strength of a syrup (120 mg per 5 ml), the child's age or weight, "as needed", "before/after food", or whether a drug was prescribed now or only taken in the past. Without these fields three CRITICAL error classes (CR-ROUTE, CR-PAEDIATRIC, CR-LIQUID-STRENGTH) can never be measured. How do we add them without breaking the eight modules that already use v1?
- Options we considered: (1) Change record.v1 in place. Pro: one format. Con: every module and test breaks at once; the brief forbids changing a contract without a version bump. (2) Add an optional "extensions" object to v1. Pro: nothing breaks. Con: the safety fields would be optional and easy to forget; v1 would no longer mean one thing. (3) A new version, record.v2 (and gold.v2, because the scorer compares records with gold), living NEXT TO v1. Pro: nothing existing breaks; modules move one at a time; the new fields are required (null when not said), so they cannot be silently forgotten. Con: two versions exist for a while; a converter is needed.
- What we chose: Option 3. Per medication: route, strength_value + strength_unit (separate from the dose amount), formulation, as_needed, food_timing, status, speaker and field_evidence (one evidence span per filled-in new field). Per advice: speaker. Per record: patient_age_band and weight_kg, each either null or a value with evidence.
- Why: A record that says "ceftriaxone 1000 mg" without "injection", or "10 ml" without the syrup strength, can mislead a prescriber. Putting the fields in a new version fixes the root cause while the old version keeps working. The bare name "record" still means v1, so no module changes meaning by accident.
- What we actually did: contracts/schemas/record.v2.schema.json, contracts/schemas/gold.v2.schema.json; new rules in contracts/validate.py; fixtures in fixtures/record.v2/ (11 valid, 9 invalid) and fixtures/gold.v2/ (9 valid, 5 invalid), generated by fixtures/build_fixtures.py, including a child syrup example, an injection example and a past-tense "already taken" example; tests/test_record_v2.py (43 tests). Field table and per-module notes: contracts/README.md. Run `uv run python -m contracts.validate --check-fixtures fixtures` and `uv run pytest tests -q`.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Until m3, m4, m5, m6 and the m7 scorer switch to v2 the three classes stay "not measurable". The fixture doses and Kannada sentences are illustrative, not native-validated and not clinically checked. The clinical review that asked for these fields is ai_simulated_review, not a real clinician.
- What else could be done: Add a paediatric weight-based dose table from a fetched national source so the verifier can really check child doses; add a "dose_basis" (per kg) field later if a real clinician asks for it.
- How to change this decision later: add record.v3 the same way (new schema file, CHANGELOG entry, fixtures, migrator); never edit a published version.
- Status: done (contract); module migration not done (Phase C)

### D-126: "negated" keeps only its grammar meaning; a new "status" field says what happened with the drug   (Phase C / contracts / architect / 2026-11-12)
- What we needed to decide: In v1, `negated: true` on a drug could mean "do not take it", "it was not taken", or "the course was not finished" (D-122 finding M3-M1). A reader would assume "no exposure", which can be wrong. How should v2 say what actually happened?
- Options we considered: (1) Keep only `negated`. Pro: simple. Con: the dangerous ambiguity stays. (2) Replace `negated` by a status. Pro: one clear field. Con: the negation checks (m3 table, m4 C3) and the CR-NEG scoring use `negated`; removing it breaks them. (3) Keep `negated` for "the sentence is negated" and add `status` with the clinical meaning: prescribed, already_taken, not_taken, advised_against, stopped_early, continue_existing, unknown.
- What we chose: Option 3. We added `not_taken` to the requested list: "did the child drink the syrup? / no" is neither "advised against" nor "unknown", and the reviewer listed it.
- Why: Grammar (is there a "not"?) and clinical meaning (was it given?) are different questions; mixing them caused the bug. Any status other than "unknown" must point at the words that justify it.
- What we actually did: `status` and its evidence rule in record.v2/gold.v2 and validate.py; fixture rec2-fx-asha-002 (not_taken, cross-turn "ಇಲ್ಲ"), rec2-fx-past-009 (already_taken and advised_against). The severity note says which status mix-ups count as CR-NEG.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Deciding status from Kannada past tense or a "-ಬೇಡಿ" ending is a language rule that is not native-validated. Some cases may fit two statuses; then "unknown" is the honest answer.
- What else could be done: ask a real clinician whether "partly taken" needs its own value.
- How to change this decision later: add a value to the status enum in a new schema version; update docs/severity.md mapping note.
- Status: done

### D-127: Reject hidden text-direction characters (bidi controls) in v2 records   (Phase C / contracts / architect / 2026-11-12)
- What we needed to decide: The m6 security audit (D-123, finding F6) showed that Unicode "bidi control" characters (U+202A..U+202E, U+2066..U+2069) can make text DISPLAY differently from what is stored, for example "after food" shown where "before food" is stored. Where do we stop them?
- Options we considered: (1) Only in the UI. Pro: small. Con: the API, database and scorer would still accept them. (2) In the contract validator for every v2 document. Pro: every module that validates gets the protection. Con: a real need for these characters (right-to-left scripts) would be blocked; Kannada and English never need them. (3) Also force it on v1. Con: changes v1 behaviour while other agents rely on it.
- What we chose: Option 2, plus an OPT-IN function for v1 (`bidi_errors(doc)` or `validate_doc(..., reject_bidi=True)`) so m5 and m6 can use it now.
- Why: Our text is Kannada and English only, so these characters have no honest use; blocking them in one shared place is cheap and closes a display trick.
- What we actually did: contracts/validate.py (`BIDI_CONTROLS`, `bidi_errors`, always on for record.v2 and gold.v2); attack fixtures fixtures/record.v2/invalid/bidi_override_in_advice_text.json, bidi_isolate_in_drug.json and fixtures/gold.v2/invalid/bidi_isolate_in_turn_text.json (written as \u escapes so the files themselves display honestly); 11 bidi tests.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Other invisible characters (U+200E/U+200F direction marks, U+061C) are NOT blocked because they were not in the finding; they cannot reorder text the same way but can still confuse. The UI must still escape text (XSS is a separate issue).
- What else could be done: block all Unicode "format" characters except ZWJ/ZWNJ, after checking that no Kannada text needs them.
- How to change this decision later: `BIDI_CONTROLS` in contracts/validate.py (with a CHANGELOG entry).
- Status: done (v2); opt-in for v1 (m5/m6 must call it)

### D-128: Small additions and conventions in record.v2 (injection without IV/IM, speaker roles, strength versus dose, age bands)   (Phase C / contracts / architect / 2026-11-12)
- What we needed to decide: The requested lists left a few real cases with no honest value, and some fields need a writing convention so m1, m3 and the scorer fill them the same way.
- Options we considered: (1) Use only the requested values and squeeze other cases into "other" or "unknown". Pro: exactly as asked. Con: "injection" said without IV/IM would become "unknown" and lose the fact that it is not oral; a nurse or ASHA would be recorded as "doctor" or "unknown". (2) Add a few clearly named values and document them. Pro: honest records. Con: a slightly longer list to learn.
- What we chose: Option 2. Route adds `injection_unspecified`. Speaker adds `nurse`, `asha`, `pharmacist` (gold role "physician" maps to "doctor"). Conventions: dose = amount per time given (5 ml, 1 tablet, 500 mg, as in v1); strength = product strength only when said as such (120 mg/5 ml, or "500 mg, two tablets"); route null when not said even if a syrup implies oral; age bands infant < 1 y, child 1-9 y, adolescent 10-17 y, adult 18-64 y, older_adult >= 65 y; "ಮಗು" without an age = child.
- Why: The verifier can only flag what the record can express. Keeping dose with its v1 meaning keeps old scores comparable.
- What we actually did: enums and descriptions in both v2 schemas; conventions in contracts/README.md; fixture rec2-fx-inj-008 uses injection_unspecified.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: The age-band cut-offs are an architect choice, UNVERIFIED (no source fetched). "ಮಗು = child" may hide an infant. Whether "500 mg tablet" should also fill strength is a judgement call for a clinician.
- What else could be done: take age bands from a fetched WHO or national guideline; ask a pharmacist about strength versus dose.
- How to change this decision later: new schema version plus contracts/README.md conventions.
- Status: needs human (age bands and strength convention to confirm)

### D-129: A careful converter from v1 to v2, and the order in which modules move   (Phase C / contracts / architect / 2026-11-12)
- What we needed to decide: Existing v1 records and gold files must keep working while modules move to v2 one by one. How do we convert them, and what should the converter guess?
- Options we considered: (1) Guess new fields from v1 (for example negated -> "advised_against", syrup -> oral). Pro: richer converted data. Con: a guess becomes a clinical claim nobody made. (2) Convert conservatively: new fields null, status "unknown", speaker "unknown" for records (transcripts have no speaker roles), speaker from the turn role for gold (that is exact). Pro: never invents. Con: converted data cannot measure the new classes.
- What we chose: Option 2, with a check that the input is valid v1 and the output valid v2 (it refuses otherwise).
- Why: A converter that guesses would hide exactly the errors v2 is meant to expose.
- What we actually did: contracts/migrate_v1_to_v2.py (`migrate_record`, `migrate_gold`, CLI `uv run python -m contracts.migrate_v1_to_v2 record in.jsonl -o out.jsonl`); 5 tests; one migrated fixture. Migration order in contracts/README.md: m3 extract, m4 checks (C4 route/paediatric/liquid strength), m5 DB columns, m6 display, m7 scorer severity mapping, m1 gold.v2.
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: Converted records look complete but carry no route/strength/age; reports must say "not measurable" for them (written into docs/severity.md).
- What else could be done: re-extract with m3 instead of converting once m3 emits v2.
- How to change this decision later: contracts/migrate_v1_to_v2.py and its tests in tests/test_record_v2.py.
- Status: done (tool); module migration not done

### D-130: Landing a contract change while another agent runs the full test suite   (Phase C / contracts / architect / 2026-11-12)
- What we needed to decide: An audio agent was running the whole test suite while this change was made. Three things could break it: (a) a test said "exactly eight schemas"; (b) the fixture script deleted and rewrote every fixtures folder, so a test reading fixtures at that moment could fail; (c) the m3 "no test-only words" guard counts every fixture word as dev-visible, so a new fixture word that appears only in test templates would quietly weaken it.
- Options we considered: (1) Edit files in place. Con: half-finished states visible to the other agent. (2) Build and test everything in a private copy, then copy in, in a safe order (validator and fixtures before schemas, new test last).
- What we chose: Option 2, plus: the "eight schemas" test now accepts numbered later versions (it still requires all eight v1); the fixture script writes only changed files and never deletes a folder; every new Kannada fixture word was checked against the test-only word list (none were test-only).
- Why: "Nothing existing may break" includes the moment of the change, not only the end state.
- What we actually did: tests/test_contracts.py (one test loosened, still strict on v1), fixtures/build_fixtures.py (`sync_dir`, re-run reports "files changed: 0"); v1 fixture files are byte-identical (checked with diff).
- What could go wrong / weaknesses: The full suite had one collection error in the audio agent's unfinished m1_data/tests/test_audio_build.py (module not written yet); that is theirs, not caused by this change.
- What else could be done: a CI step that fails if build_fixtures.py would change any committed fixture.
- How to change this decision later: fixtures/build_fixtures.py, tests/test_contracts.py.
- Status: done

### D-131: TTS model: MMS-TTS Kannada, because Indic Parler-TTS is gated   (Phase B / m1_data / data-scientist / 2026-11-12)
- What we needed to decide: Which text-to-speech model makes our synthetic test audio, when the brief's first choice (AI4Bharat Indic Parler-TTS) cannot be downloaded.
- Options we considered:
